# Document 1: NetraAskHRRetrievalLLD_16SeptFromVarshaV2.pdf

## Ask HR — Retrieval & Answer: Low-Level Design

This LLD specifies the full path a question takes: embed, check the curated cache, filter, search, grade, corrective retrieval, rerank, expand, score retrieval strength, gather evidence, generate the answer, score answer confidence, screen, and return. The orchestrator drives the sequence and applies the confidence bar; the retrieval and answer services do the work.

### Part A — The flow (step by step)

The orchestrator runs these steps in order. Steps 2 and 6 can short-circuit the rest (a cache hit, or an unanswerable question). Each step is detailed in Part B.

| # | Step | Owner | Output | Next / short-circuit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Embed the question | Retrieval | query vector | reused in steps 2 and 4 |
| 2 | Curated cache lookup | Cache | hit or miss | hit -> step 10 (return); miss -> 3 |
| 3 | Build access + currency filter | Access + Retrieval | the search filter | always -> 4 |
| 4 | Vector search | Retrieval | ~25 chunks (Configurable) | -> 5 |
| 5 | Grade the chunks | Retrieval | grade 0-1 | >= min_grade -> 7; else 6 |
| 6 | Corrective retrieval | Retrieval | rewritten query, re-search | max 2, then no-answer |
| 7 | Rerank + keep top 3 | Retrieval | 3 best chunks | -> 8 |
| 8 | Parent expansion | Retrieval | chunks + parent sections | -> 9 |
| 9 | retrieval_strength | Retrieval | strength 0-1 | -> 10 evidence |
| 10 | Assemble evidence + provenance | Retrieval (code) | text + source + page + version + links | -> 11 |
| 11 | Generate answer | Answer | grounded, cited answer | -> 12 |
| 12 | Score answer confidence | Answer | confidence 0-1 | orchestrator applies 0.90 bar |
| 13 | Output guardrail | Guardrail | pass / block | -> 14 |
| 14 | Return + write audit | Orchestrator | response + audit record | done |

### Part A2 — Component inventory

What this flow is made of, so the build can be planned. One agent, six services, the tools inside them, the external models, and the stores.

#### Agent (1)

- HR domain agent — supplies retrieval config (collection, filters, thresholds, prompt id) and holds the domain knowledge. The orchestrator is the driver, not an agent.

#### Services (6)

| Service | Steps it owns |
|---|---|
| Orchestrator | drives all 14 steps, applies bars, short-circuits, writes audit |
| Retrieval service | 1, 3-10 |
| Answer service | 11-12 |
| Cache service | 2 |
| Access service | 3 (access scope) |
| Guardrail service | 13 |

#### Tools / components inside the services (12)

| Tool / component | Step | In service |
|---|---|---|
| Embedder | 1 | Retrieval |
| Cache matcher | 2 | Cache |
| Filter builder | 3 | Access + Retrieval |
| Vector searcher | 4 | Retrieval |
| Grader | 5 | Retrieval |
| Query rewriter (4 strategies) | 6 | Retrieval |
| Reranker client | 7 | Retrieval |
| Parent expander | 8 | Retrieval |
| Strength calculator | 9 | Retrieval |
| Evidence + provenance assembler | 10 | Retrieval |
| Answer generator + citation resolver | 11 | Answer |
| Confidence + groundedness scorer | 12 | Answer |

#### External model / guard services (4)

- Embedding model (Baxter self-hosted, should be in the pilot code) — step 1. Reranker model (self-hosted) — step 7. LLM gateway, Claude via Baxter AI Hub — step 11. BaxiGuard content-policy service — step 13.

#### Stores (3)

- Qdrant (vectors) — steps 2, 4. PostgreSQL (registry, provenance, audit) — steps 10, 14. Object store (downloadable files) — step 10.

### Part B — Each step in detail

#### Step 1 — Embed the question

- The (already history-rewritten, if a follow-up) question is embedded once, using the same model and version used on the documents, so query and chunk vectors are comparable.
- The vector is reused for the cache lookup (step 2) and the search (step 4). It is never computed twice.

```text
def embed(text) -> EmbedResult:   # vector, model_id, model_version, tokens
```

#### Step 2 — Curated cache lookup

- The query vector is matched against the curated-answer vectors in the cache collection.
- A cache entry is served only when both conditions hold: the similarity is at or above the bar, and the entry's access_scope equals the caller's access_scope.

> **Rule:** Serve a curated answer only if similarity >= cache_similarity_bar (0.95) AND access_scope matches. On a hit, skip steps 3-12 and go straight to return (step 10 provenance is already stored on the curated entry). On a miss, continue to step 3.

- Only human-approved curated answers are in the cache. Live answers are never auto-cached, so a served cache answer is always one a person approved.

#### Step 3 — Build the access + currency filter (deterministic)

Before searching, the orchestrator gets the caller's access scope from the Access service and the retrieval config from the domain handler, and builds one filter applied inside the search.

```text
filter.must = [
  is_current == true,
  effective_from <= today <= effective_to,
  audience_match(user.country, user.audience, user.department)  OR  audience == 'general' ]
if topic_confident: filter.must += [ topic == detected_topic ]
```

> **Rule:** Access is enforced here by hard metadata match, never by the model. The filter is applied in the search, not after results return, so restricted or stale content is never retrieved.

#### Step 4 — Vector search

- The filtered set is searched by vector similarity: the chunks whose vectors are closest to the query vector are returned.
- Return candidate_count chunks (default 25) for the later steps to refine.

> **Rule:** Vector search only in Phase 1. Config: candidate_count = 25.

> **Note:** *Keyword (sparse) search is deliberately NOT included in Phase 1. Vector search is sufficient for well-structured HR prose, and adding a second index plus score-merging is complexity that should be added only if the evaluation set shows exact-term misses (a form number, a specific policy name). This is the standard start-simple approach; keyword search is a fast follow if the data proves it is needed.*

#### Step 5 — Grade the chunks

- The system scores whether the returned chunks actually cover the question, from the top similarity scores, the spread between them, and how many cleared a minimum.
- This produces a grade between 0 and 1.

> **Rule:** If grade >= min_grade (0.55), go to rerank (step 7). If below, go to corrective retrieval (step 6). Config: min_grade = 0.55.

#### Step 6 — Corrective retrieval (only when the grade is weak)

The system rewrites the question and searches again rather than answering from weak chunks. Strategies are applied in order.

- Expansion — add synonyms and related terms (PTO = paid time off, vacation, annual leave).
- Decomposition — split a question that still holds two asks.
- HyDE — draft a hypothetical answer and search on it; draft answers match document wording better than questions.
- Step-back — broaden first for context, then narrow.

> **Rule:** At most max_rewrites (2) rewrites. If the grade is still below min_grade after that, retrieval returns an empty result and the answer is a clean no-answer. A no-answer is a correct outcome. Config: max_rewrites = 2, enabled_rewrites = [expansion, decomposition].

#### Step 7 — Rerank and keep the top 3

- A cross-encoder re-scores each chunk against the question directly — more accurate than the vector-search score, which is why search narrows to ~25 first.
- Keep keep_count (3) best chunks.

> **Rule:** If the reranker is unavailable, keep the top chunks in raw search order and set reranked = false; answer confidence is penalised (step 12). Config: keep_count = 3.

#### Step 8 — Parent expansion

- Each kept chunk is expanded to its parent section (the parent link set at ingestion), so the answer step has enough context. The small chunk matched; the parent is what is passed on.

#### Step 9 — retrieval_strength

- A single 0-1 score summarising how well the kept sections match the question, from the rerank scores of the kept chunks: mainly the top score, adjusted for consistency of the rest. Recorded on the request; it is one of the two inputs to answer confidence.

#### Step 10 — Assemble evidence and provenance (by code)

The system gathers, deterministically in code, everything needed to answer and cite — the model does not build the citation trail.

```text
Evidence = [ {
  text,                    # the parent section
  document_title,
  source_file,
  page_or_slide,           # page (PDF) or slide (PPT)
  version, effective_from, effective_to,
  source_link,             # opens the document at the right place
  downloadable, download_link, download_label } ]
```

> **Rule:** Provenance — source file, page or slide number, version, links — is captured from what retrieval actually returned. The model does not invent citations. Every answer traces to the exact approved content it used.

#### Step 11 — Generate the answer

##### Prompt

- Stable system block (grounding rules: answer only from the evidence, quote numbers exactly, cite sections used, decline if not covered), the evidence sections with source labels, trimmed conversation context, the question, and the output-format instruction.
- The system block is stable across calls so it bills at the cached-prompt rate.

##### Output

- A short grounded answer plus the ids of the sections used. Code resolves those ids to the visible citations using the step-10 evidence.
- If no sections were used, the result is treated as a no-answer, not a fabricated answer.

```text
def generate(GenerateRequest) -> GenerateResult:
  # answer, sources_used, cost(tokens_in, cached, out, usd), model_id, model_version
```

#### Step 12 — Score answer confidence, and the bar

Answer confidence is computed by the system, not asked of the model.

- Input 1: retrieval_strength (step 9) — how well the evidence matched.
- Input 2: groundedness — each claim in the answer is checked against the cited evidence; does the answer follow from the sources.
- A penalty applies if reranked = false.
- These combine into a single confidence score with its basis recorded.

> **Rule:** The orchestrator shows the answer only if confidence >= 0.90. A single, strict bar for every answer: an ungrounded or wrong HR answer is worse than no answer. Below 0.90 the answer is withheld and the user gets a no-answer, one clarifying question, or a ticket offer. Config: answer_confidence_bar = 0.90 (tuned against the golden test set).

#### Step 13 — Output guardrail

- The complete answer is screened by the guardrail (BaxiGuard) before any of it is shown. A blocked answer becomes a refusal, not the answer. Output is screened in full before display (no partial-stream exposure in Phase 1).

#### Step 14 — Return and write audit

- The response returns: answer, citations (document, page/slide, version), download links where applicable, and — for admins — the confidence.
- Before responding, the orchestrator writes the audit record: question, answer, sources used (references, not copied text), confidence and basis, models and versions, retrieval details, outcome.

> **Rule:** An answer is never returned if the audit could not be written. Audit is fail-closed.

### Part B2 — Data model (retrieval-relevant tables)

The tables this flow reads and writes, at column level. Chunk text and vectors live in Qdrant; everything below is PostgreSQL. Types are indicative (Postgres).

#### messages — the audit + chat-history record (append-only)

One row per request. This is both the audit trail and the user's chat history (viewed with different scope). Append-only: no update, no delete.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| message_id | uuid PK |  |
| conversation_id | uuid FK | groups a thread |
| user_id | text | who asked; scopes chat-history view |
| asked_at | timestamptz |  |
| question | text | as received |
| rewritten_question | text | after history rewrite, if any |
| status | text | ANSWERED \| NO_ANSWER \| REFUSED \| ERROR \| PARTIAL |
| answer | text | null if not answered |
| cited_sources | jsonb | references only: [{chunk_id, version_id, page_or_slide, link}] — never copied text |
| confidence | numeric(4,3) | final answer confidence |
| confidence_basis | jsonb | {retrieval_strength, groundedness, reranked_penalty} |
| retrieval_detail | jsonb | {candidates, kept, rewrites_used, reranked, tokens_retrieved} |
| route_path | text | DIRECT / CACHE / ESCALATED etc. |
| cache_hit | boolean |  |
| models_used | jsonb | [{role, model_id, model_version}] |
| guardrail_in | text | ALLOW \| BLOCK |
| guardrail_out | text | ALLOW \| BLOCK |
| cost | jsonb | {tokens_in, tokens_in_cached, tokens_out, usd} |
| error_code | text | null unless ERROR |

> **Rule:** cited_sources stores references, not source text. Copying text would bloat records 10-20x for no benefit and duplicate what the chunk/version rows already hold.

#### chunks — citation + provenance target

The row a citation points at. The chunk text and embedding live in Qdrant under the same chunk_id; this row is the durable reference.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| chunk_id | text PK | also the Qdrant point id |
| document_id | uuid FK |  |
| version_id | uuid FK | which document version |
| parent_section_id | text | for parent expansion (step 8) |
| heading_path | text | e.g. Leave > Carry-over |
| page_or_slide | int | page (PDF) or slide (PPT) |
| locator | text | anchor within the document |
| link | text | opens the document at this place |

#### documents / document_versions — currency & version history

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| document_id | uuid PK | documents: one per document |
| current_version_id | uuid FK | pointer to the live version |
| version_id | uuid PK | document_versions: one per version |
| version_label | text | e.g. 2025.1 |
| is_current | boolean | exactly one true per document (enforced) |
| effective_from | date | step 3 filters on this |
| effective_to | date | null = open-ended |
| title, country, audience, department, topic, access_tag | various | the human metadata (drives filtering) |
| downloadable, download_link, download_label | bool/text | for downloadable documents (step 10) |
| approved_by, approved_at | text/ts | the approval gate |

> **Rule:** A partial-unique constraint enforces exactly one is_current = true per document_id. Step 3 filters on is_current + effective dates; step 10 cites version_id.

#### cache_entries — curated answers (step 2)

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| cache_id | uuid PK | also the cache-collection point id in Qdrant |
| question_text | text | the curated question |
| answer | text | the curated answer |
| access_scope | text | hash of visibility attributes; must match caller (step 2) |
| cited_sources | jsonb | references, same shape as messages |
| authored_by, approved_by, approved_at | text/ts | approval trail |
| built_from_versions | jsonb | version_ids this answer relies on — for invalidation |
| is_active | boolean | deactivated when a source version is superseded |

> **Rule:** A curated answer is served only if its access_scope matches the caller and is_active is true. When a version in built_from_versions is superseded, the entry is deactivated (step in the publish transaction).

#### conversations — thread grouping (for history rewrite)

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| conversation_id | uuid PK |  |
| user_id | text |  |
| started_at, last_activity_at | timestamptz | session window / topic boundary |

Supporting tables defined in the full data model (not detailed here as this flow only reads them): feedback, user_attributes, pending_context, tickets.

### Part C — Where this sits (orchestrator handoff)

- The orchestrator (the supervisor/LangGraph flow) owns the sequence. It calls the Retrieval service for steps 1 and 3-10, the Answer service for steps 11-12, the Guardrail for step 13, and writes the audit at step 14.
- The Retrieval and Answer services hold no domain knowledge and no control flow decisions — the domain handler supplies config (collection, filters, thresholds, prompt id); the orchestrator applies the bars and short-circuits.
- Retrieval runs per sub-query: if the router produced more than one sub-query, steps 3-10 run for each, and the results are merged before answer generation.

```text
def retrieve(sub_query, vector, access_scope, config) -> RetrievalResult:
  # evidence[], retrieval_strength, rewrites_used, reranked, tokens_retrieved
```

### Part C2 — Code structure & isolation

Where retrieval and answer code physically lives, and how it stays separate from the existing pilot until a clean merge later.

#### Folder layout

```text
Netra/backend/
  core/            config (settings), logging, shared types
  orchestrator/    the flow driver (the 14 steps)
  retrieval/       embed, filter, search, grade, rewrite, rerank,
                   parent-expand, strength, evidence assembler
  answer/          generate, citation resolver, confidence + groundedness
  cache/           curated lookup
  access/          access-scope resolution + filter build
  guardrail/       BaxiGuard adapter (in/out screening)
  stores/          qdrant client, postgres client, object store
  models/          embedding, reranker, llm gateway clients
  tests/
```

#### Isolation rules (so it does not touch the pilot)

- Each concern is a self-contained module under backend/, in its own namespaced folder. Retrieval logic lives only in retrieval/, answer logic only in answer/, and so on.
- No module imports or edits the existing pilot's files. This code calls Qdrant, Postgres, the models and the guardrail through its own clients in stores/ and models/, not through the pilot's code.
- Dependencies point inward to core/ only (config, logging, shared types). A service never reaches into another service's internals; it calls it through its defined interface.
- Config is thin and central (core/settings). Nothing is hardcoded, so at merge the modules re-point to the host backend's config with minimal change.

> **Rule:** This is built as independent code now and merges into the existing backend later by dropping these folders in as new modules. Because everything is namespaced under its own folder and nothing imports pilot files, the merge adds modules rather than overwriting or colliding with existing code.

#### Merge path

- Now: runs standalone against local Postgres + Qdrant + a local embedding model, with the external services (LLM gateway, guardrail, OCR) pointed at mocks or real URLs via config.
- At merge: the folders move into the host backend/; the clients in stores/ and models/ are pointed at the shared instances; config is merged into the host's settings. No retrieval/answer logic changes.

### Part D — Thresholds (all in one place)

| Where | Rule | Config |
|---|---|---|
| Curated cache | serve only if similarity >= 0.95 AND access_scope match | cache_similarity_bar = 0.95 |
| Search | return ~25 closest chunks (vector only) | candidate_count = 25 |
| Grade | proceed if >= 0.55, else corrective retrieval | min_grade = 0.55 |
| Corrective retrieval | max 2 rewrites, then no-answer | max_rewrites = 2 |
| Keep | best 3 after rerank | keep_count = 3 |
| Reranker down | raw order, confidence penalty | (fail-open) |
| Answer confidence | show only if >= 0.90 (single high bar) | answer_confidence_bar = 0.90 |

All thresholds are config and are tuned against the golden test set. Values above are the starting points.

### Part E — Errors and edge cases

| Case | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Cache miss | continue normally; not an error |
| Grade weak after 2 rewrites | empty result; clean no-answer, never a guess |
| Reranker unavailable | raw order kept, confidence penalised (fail-open) |
| Embedding or vector store unavailable | fail closed; request errors, no answer |
| Model used no sections | treated as no-answer, not a fabricated answer |
| Confidence below 0.90 | answer withheld; clarify or ticket offer |
| Output blocked by guardrail | refusal message, not the answer |
| Audit cannot be written | answer not returned (fail-closed) |

### Part F — Design decisions on record

- Vector search only in Phase 1; keyword search deferred and added only if the eval set shows exact-term misses. Start simple; add complexity the data proves is needed.
- Single strict answer bar (0.90) for all answers — a wrong HR answer is worse than no answer.
- Provenance captured by code, not the model, so citations are always real and traceable.
- Access enforced by deterministic filter, never by the model.
- No-answer is a first-class outcome, designed for, not an error.
