# Document 2: AskHR_Ingestion_LLD.docx

## Ask HR — Ingestion: Low-Level Design

*Extractors, metadata, bulk load, downloadable documents, error handling, and the ingestion UI*

*Component-level design for developers. Medium depth: classes, signatures, logic, config, errors. Version 1.0.*

Ingestion brings documents into the system so they can be answered from. It runs offline, separate from answering, so loading never competes with live questions. This document specifies how it is built, including the human metadata file, the staged bulk load, downloadable documents, and the admin UI.

### Part A — Structure and shared pipeline

#### A1. One framework, source-type handlers

- There is one ingestion framework. Each source type (PDF, PowerPoint, and later intranet pages, schema metadata) has its own handler behind a common interface.
- The pipeline stages are shared: change detection, normalize, chunk, tag, approve, embed, publish. Only extraction and chunking vary by type.
- Handlers are registered by source type. Adding a type is adding a handler, not a new pipeline.

```text
registries/extractors.py
EXTRACTORS: dict[str, Extractor] = {}   # 'pdf' -> PdfExtractor, 'pptx' -> PptxExtractor
def register(fmt): ...                   # decorator
def get_extractor(fmt) -> Extractor:
    if fmt not in EXTRACTORS: raise UnsupportedFormat(fmt)
    return EXTRACTORS[fmt]
```

#### A2. The common interface

```text
class Extractor(Protocol):
    def supports(self, path: str) -> bool: ...
    def extract(self, path: str) -> NormalizedDocument: ...

class NormalizedDocument(BaseModel):
    document_id: str; source_path: str; fmt: str; fingerprint: str
    language: str
    blocks: list[Block]          # HEADING | PARAGRAPH | TABLE | LIST | IMAGE

class Block(BaseModel):
    type: str; text: str; level: int | None
    page_or_slide: int; ocr_text: str | None; table_rows: list[list[str]] | None
```

> **Rule:** Every extractor returns the same NormalizedDocument. Everything after extraction works on blocks and never on a specific file format.

### Part B — Extractors

#### B1. PDF extractor

##### Logic

1. Open the file. If it is password-protected or corrupt, raise ExtractionFailed and quarantine.
2. Detect whether it has a real text layer or is scanned images.
3. Text PDF: extract text with layout (PyMuPDF), preserving headings and reading order.
4. Scanned PDF: run OCR page by page, then treat the OCR text as the content.
5. Tables: detect and extract as rows; a table spanning pages is stitched using the repeated header row.
6. Images with embedded text: OCR them; purely decorative images are ignored.
7. Emit the NormalizedDocument with page numbers on every block.

##### Text vs scanned, and the OCR service

- PyMuPDF is the default for text-based PDFs: local, fast, no external call.
- Scanned or image-only PDFs have no text layer. They are sent to an external OCR/extraction service.
- That service is Baxi Extract (Baxter's wrapper over Azure Document Intelligence), called by a configurable URL. The pilot already integrates it.

> **Rule:** The OCR/extraction service is a configurable URL and auth reference, never hardcoded. It is called through an adapter, so it can be pointed at Baxi Extract, at a mock for local testing, or at another service, by config alone. Local parsing (PyMuPDF) needs no URL; only OCR does.

##### Signature and config

```text
class PdfExtractor(Extractor):
    def extract(self, path) -> NormalizedDocument: ...
        # text PDF -> PyMuPDF (local)
        # scanned  -> ExtractServiceClient (configurable URL)

class ExtractServiceClient:              # adapter over the OCR service
    def extract(self, file_bytes) -> str: ...   # upload + poll until done

config: extract_service_url, extract_service_auth_ref,
        ocr_language, max_pages, table_detection_on
```

#### B2. PowerPoint extractor

##### Logic

1. Open with python-pptx. If unreadable, raise ExtractionFailed and quarantine.
2. For each slide: read the title, the body text, and the speaker notes.
3. Text baked into slide images is passed through OCR.
4. Emit one block group per slide, tagged with the slide number; one slide becomes one chunk downstream.

##### Signature

```text
class PptxExtractor(Extractor):
    def extract(self, path) -> NormalizedDocument: ...
```

> **Rule:** PowerPoint is parsed natively, never converted to PDF. Conversion loses speaker notes and the clean slide boundary.

#### B2b. Excel extractor  (row-level, not prose)

Excel is tabular data, not prose. Chunking it like a document tears rows apart and detaches values from their headers, which makes retrieval useless. It gets its own handler and its own chunking.

##### Logic

1. Open the workbook. Read each sheet.
2. Treat the first row (or a detected header row) as the column headers.
3. Each data row becomes one chunk, with the column headers attached to its values.
4. A row [India, Manager, 20, 15] under headers [Country, Role, Annual, Carry-over] becomes a chunk: ‘Country: India · Role: Manager · Annual: 20 · Carry-over: 15’.
5. Very wide sheets, or logical groups of rows, may be grouped into one chunk (config), but a value is never separated from its header.
6. Sheet name and row number are kept as machine metadata for citation.

##### Why row-level

- So a value carries its meaning. “15” alone is noise; “Carry-over: 15” is retrievable and answerable.
- This matches the pilot, which has a dedicated Excel path (excel_ingestor / excel_row_chunker) separate from documents.

##### Signature

```text
class ExcelExtractor(Extractor):
    def extract(self, path) -> NormalizedDocument: ...
        # one chunk per row, headers attached to each value

config: excel_header_row_detect, excel_rows_per_chunk
```

> **Rule:** Excel rows are chunked with their headers attached, one row (or a small group) per chunk. It is a distinct handler registered for the xlsx source type; the framework is the same, the chunker differs.

#### B3. Chunking

- Chunking is specified in detail in Part B2. In summary: the chunker picks a strategy from the document structure, splits structure-first with a size cap and sentence-level overlap, keeps tables and lists whole, and prepends a context label to every chunk.

```text
def chunk(doc: NormalizedDocument, cfg: ChunkConfig) -> list[Chunk]:
    """Select a strategy from the document blocks (see Part B2) and return chunks."""
```

### Part B2 — Chunking design (detailed)

Chunking decides what gets embedded and retrieved, so it decides answer quality more than almost anything else. This part specifies it at an implementable level, not just the principles.

#### BC1. Strategy is chosen per document structure

- There is not one chunking strategy. The chunker inspects the normalized document and picks the right approach for its structure.

| Document shape | Chunking approach |
|---|---|
| Has clear headings (most policies) | Section-based: split on headings, one section = one chunk group, cap-and-overlap within a long section. |
| Flat, no headings | Sliding window: fixed-size windows with overlap, since there are no natural boundaries. |
| Table-heavy (benefits grids) | Table-aware: each table is its own chunk; surrounding text chunks separately. |
| Slides (PPTX) | One slide = one chunk, title + body + notes together. |
| List-heavy | Keep a list with its introducing sentence; do not split a list from its heading. |

> **Rule:** The chunker selects the approach from the document's blocks, not from the file type. A PDF with no headings uses the flat strategy; a PDF with headings uses the section strategy.

#### BC2. Heading detection (the hard part for PDF)

Word and PowerPoint carry explicit heading styles. PDFs usually do not, so headings are inferred.

- Signals used, combined: font size larger than body, bold weight, a short line followed by a paragraph, numbering patterns (1., 1.1, A.), and consistent left position.
- A line is treated as a heading when several signals agree, not on one alone.
- The detected heading level (H1/H2/H3) comes from relative font size and numbering depth.
- If heading detection finds nothing, the document is treated as flat and the sliding-window strategy is used.

> **Open:** *Heading detection is heuristic and imperfect. The extractor toolkit (evaluate Unstructured.io / Docling) may provide this; if so, use it rather than hand-building. Record the choice.*

#### BC3. Section-based chunking (documents with headings)

1. Group blocks under their heading into a section.
2. If a section is within the size cap, it is one chunk.
3. If a section exceeds the cap, split it at paragraph boundaries into multiple chunks, each carrying the same heading label, with overlap between them.
4. Never split mid-paragraph or mid-sentence.
5. The heading path (for example Leave > Carry-over) is stored on every chunk of that section.

#### BC4. The size cap and overlap

- Target chunk size about 1,500 characters, roughly 300 words. This is a target, not a hard cut mid-content.
- Overlap about 15 percent, applied only when a section is split into multiple chunks. The overlap is whole trailing sentences of the previous chunk repeated at the start of the next, so context is not lost at the boundary.
- Overlap is by sentence, never by raw character count, so a chunk never starts mid-sentence.

```text
class ChunkConfig(BaseModel):
    target_chars: int = 1500
    overlap_pct: float = 0.15        # applied only on intra-section splits
    overlap_unit: str = sentence     # never mid-sentence
    min_chunk_chars: int = 200       # below this, merge with neighbour
    hard_max_chars: int = 3000       # never exceed, even for a table
```

#### BC5. Tables and lists

- A table is one chunk. It is serialised to text with the header row repeated on each row group, so the meaning survives embedding.
- A table larger than the hard maximum is split by rows, with the header repeated on each part.
- A list is kept with the sentence that introduces it. A list is never split from its heading or lead-in.

#### BC6. Parent-child structure

Small chunks are embedded for precise matching; a larger parent section is returned as context, so the model has enough to answer.

- Each chunk records its parent_section_id.
- The parent is the full section the chunk came from, capped at a larger size (for example three times the chunk cap) so it stays usable.
- At retrieval, a matched small chunk is expanded to its parent before the answer is written.

```text
class Chunk(BaseModel):
    chunk_id: str; text: str; context_label: str
    parent_section_id: str; heading_path: str; page_or_slide: int
    char_count: int; token_count: int
```

#### BC7. The context label

- A one-line label is prepended to every chunk before embedding, for example “India Leave Policy · Carry-over · v2024”.
- Built from the document title, the heading path and the version.

> **Rule:** A chunk reading “employees get 15 days” is meaningless without its label. The context label is the single largest quality gain in chunking and is mandatory on every chunk.

#### BC8. Edge cases

- A section smaller than the minimum is merged with its neighbour, so the store is not filled with tiny fragments.
- A section larger than the hard maximum is force-split at paragraph boundaries even if that breaks the section.
- An empty or whitespace-only block is dropped.
- A document that produces zero usable chunks is quarantined, not published.

#### BC9. Schema chunking is different (structured data, later)

- For structured-data ingestion the unit is not prose. A chunk is a table or a column-group definition: the table name, its business description, its columns and their meanings.
- This is a separate chunking strategy registered for the schema source type. The framework is the same; the chunker differs.

### Part C — The human metadata file

#### C1. What it is

- Machine metadata is derived automatically during extraction (path, page/slide, headings, format, language, dates where present).
- Human metadata is the business knowledge a person must supply. It comes from one file, one row per document.

#### C2. Columns

| Column | Required | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| full_path | Yes | Absolute path to the file. The unique key linking the row to the file. |
| title | Yes | Display name. |
| country_region | Yes | Controlled vocabulary. Drives access. |
| audience | Yes | Controlled vocabulary. Drives access. |
| department | No | Controlled vocabulary. |
| topic | Yes | Short controlled value. AI may pre-suggest; human confirms. |
| access_tag | Yes | Controlled vocabulary. |
| version | No | Version label, e.g. 2024.2. |
| effective_from` / `effective_to | No | Currency dates. |
| owner | Yes | HR owner of the document. |
| approved_by` / `approved_date | On approval | Who approved and when. |
| downloadable | Yes | Yes or No. Whether users may download the file. |
| download_label | If downloadable | What to call it in the answer, e.g. “JD Template — Sales Manager”. |

- A separate Vocabulary sheet holds the allowed values. The controlled fields use dropdowns bound to it, so typos and variants cannot enter.

> **Rule:** full_path is the unique key; duplicates are an error. It is absolute, so multi-level folder structures are supported.

#### C3. Reading and validating the file

```text
def load_metadata(path: str) -> dict[str, DocMetadata]:
    """Return metadata indexed by full_path. Raises on structural errors."""

class DocMetadata(BaseModel):
    full_path: str; title: str; country_region: str; audience: str
    department: str | None; topic: str; access_tag: str
    version: str | None; effective_from: date | None; effective_to: date | None
    owner: str; downloadable: bool; download_label: str | None

def validate_metadata(row: DocMetadata, vocab: Vocabulary) -> list[ValidationError]:
    """Every controlled value checked against the vocabulary. Returns all errors."""
```

- Missing required column in the file: stop the run, report which column.
- A value not in the vocabulary: reject that row, name the field and the bad value.
- Duplicate full_path: error, must be unique.
- downloadable = Yes but download_label empty: error.

### Part D — Downloadable documents

#### D1. Design

- Some documents are downloaded by the user, not only answered from — templates and forms. These carry downloadable = Yes.
- When downloadable is Yes, ingestion copies the file into the object store and records a served download link on the document.
- The copy is refreshed on re-ingestion when the source changes (fingerprint detects it).

> **Rule:** Downloadable files are served from our object store, not linked to the original, so the download always works and does not depend on the user's access to the source system.

#### D2. In the answer

- When a matched source is downloadable, the answer shows two links: the source citation (where the answer came from) and the download link (the served copy), labelled with download_label.
- Non-downloadable sources show only the citation.

```text
class Source(BaseModel):
    label: str; locator: str; version: str; link: str
    downloadable: bool = False; download_link: str | None; download_label: str | None
```

- If downloadable = Yes but the file cannot be served, the document is flagged and cannot be published.

### Part E — The bulk load (staged, fully logged)

#### E1. Principle

> **Rule:** The bulk load runs in stages. Each stage validates and logs before the next begins, and the run stops at a summary for human review before anything goes live. The user sees exactly what passed, what failed, and why, at every step.

#### E2. The stages

##### Stage 1 — Validate the location

- Check the folder exists and is readable. Log the result.
- Log example: “Scanning /hr/policies — found, readable.” Or stop: “ERROR: folder not found.”

##### Stage 2 — Scan and check formats

- Walk the folders recursively; list every file; check each is a supported type.
- Log example: “Found 214 files: 180 PDF, 30 PPTX, 4 unsupported (.docx) — skipped.”

##### Stage 3 — Validate the metadata file

- Open it; check the columns; validate every controlled value against the vocabulary.
- Log example: “Metadata: 214 rows, 3 errors — row 45 country ‘Indai’ not in vocabulary; row 88 duplicate path.”

##### Stage 4 — Match files to metadata

- Every file to its row by full_path.
- Log example: “210 matched; 4 files have no metadata row (quarantined); 2 metadata rows have no file (missing).”

##### Stage 5 — Per file: extract, chunk, tag

- Each file processed and logged individually.
- Log example: “leave_policy.pdf: 12 pages, 34 chunks, OK.” / “old_form.pdf: FAILED — password-protected, quarantined.”

##### Stage 6 — Summary, hold for approval

- Show the summary; nothing is live yet.
- Log example: “Ready: 205 to publish, 9 quarantined. Review and approve.”

##### Stage 7 — On approval: embed and publish

- Only after a human approves. Downloadable files copied to the object store here.
- Log example: “Published 205 documents, 6,900 chunks.”

> **Rule:** Nothing goes live automatically, even in bulk. The log shows what is ready; a human approves. This holds for both the CLI and the UI.

#### E3. The command

A flexible CLI. Everything not a run option comes from config.

```text
python -m askhr.ingestion.run \
  --folder /path/to/hr/docs \
  --metadata /path/to/metadata.xlsx \
  --recursive \           # walk sub-folders (default on)
  --dry-run \             # screen and report only; write nothing
  --only-changed \        # skip unchanged files (fingerprint)
  --collection hr_policy \ # target collection (config default)
  --file /path/one.pdf \  # single file instead of a folder
  --report /path/run.json  # write the run report
```

- --dry-run is the safe first step: it runs stages 1 to 5 and reports, but writes nothing. Fix the metadata, then run for real.
- --collection lets the same script serve HR documents now and schema metadata later, by pointing at a different collection and metadata file.

#### E4. The run report

```text
class RunReport(BaseModel):
    run_id: str; started_at, finished_at
    location_ok: bool; folder: str
    found: int; by_format: dict[str,int]; unsupported: int
    metadata_rows: int; metadata_errors: list[RowError]
    matched: int; quarantined: list[FileError]; missing_files: list[str]
    processed: int; published: int; failed: list[FileError]
    status: str      # SUCCESS | PARTIAL | STOPPED
```

### Part F — Error handling

#### F1. Rules, at every step

- `Folder not found or unreadable` — stop the run, report.
- `Unsupported file type` — skip, list it, continue.
- `File found but no metadata row` — quarantine, log, never ingest untagged content.
- `Metadata value not in vocabulary` — reject the row, name the field and value.
- `Duplicate full_path` — error, paths must be unique.
- `Metadata row with no file on disk` — report as missing.
- `File will not extract (corrupt, password)` — quarantine with the reason, continue the run.
- `downloadable = Yes but file unservable` — flag, cannot publish.
- `File changed but not re-approved` — do not republish until approved.
- `A single file failing never stops the run` — it is quarantined and the run continues.

> **Rule:** Repeated failure on the same file across runs raises its own alert, rather than failing quietly every night.

### Part G — Publishing

#### G1. The approve-and-publish transaction

1. Chunks are written to the vector store marked not current, so retrieval cannot see them yet.
2. The write is verified.
3. On approval, in one database transaction: the old version is set not current with an effective_to date, the new version is set current with an effective_from date, and the document's current version pointer is updated.
4. In the vector store, in one batch: the new chunks are marked current and the old chunks not current.
5. Downloadable files are copied to the object store and the download link recorded.
6. Cache entries built from the old version are invalidated.
7. The old chunks are removed from the vector store; the old version's registry rows and file are kept for audit.

> **Rule:** Nothing is searchable before approval. There is no window where both versions answer, or where the document disappears.

### Part I — Metadata model, batching and execution

#### I1. The complete metadata model

Three kinds of metadata are set at ingestion. Human metadata is supplied once per document and fanned out onto every chunk, so filtering works at chunk level.

##### Machine metadata (set automatically during extraction)

```text
document_id, chunk_id, source_path, format, page_or_slide,
heading_path, language, fingerprint, parent_section_id,
char_count, token_count
```

##### Human metadata (from the metadata file, copied onto every chunk of the document)

```text
country_region, audience, department, topic, access_tag,
version, effective_from, effective_to, owner,
downloadable, download_label
```

##### System metadata (set by the pipeline)

```text
is_current, content_version, ingested_at, approved_by, approved_at,
embedding_model_id, embedding_version
```

> **Rule:** Human metadata is per document but is written onto every chunk of that document. This is deliberate: retrieval filters at chunk level, so each chunk must carry its own country, audience and currency, not point back to the document for them.

##### Where each field lives

| Field group | Postgres (registry/chunk row) | Qdrant (vector payload) |
|---|---|---|
| Machine | Yes | Yes (the filterable + provenance ones) |
| Human | Yes | Yes (all — they drive filtering) |
| System | Yes | is_current, content_version, embedding version |

- The chunk text and embedding live in Qdrant; the durable chunk record lives in Postgres; the same chunk_id links them.

#### I2. Batching at scale (hundreds to thousands of files)

- Files are processed in batches, not all at once. Batch size is config (default 50 files).
- Within a file, chunks are embedded in batches, not one at a time. Embedding thousands of chunks individually is slow; batch size is config (default 100 chunks per embed call).
- Files are streamed and released, not all held in memory, so a thousand-file run does not exhaust memory.
- Extraction can run a few files concurrently; embedding is throttled to what the GPU can handle.

#### I3. Checkpointing and re-run safety

- Each file's fingerprint and processed state are recorded as it completes.
- If a run crashes at file 400 of 1000, restarting resumes from 401. Completed files are skipped by fingerprint, not reprocessed.

> **Rule:** A re-run is safe and idempotent. Re-running the same folder and metadata does not duplicate content or re-embed unchanged files. This is what makes a large load recoverable rather than all-or-nothing.

#### I4. Version detection on update

1. A document is identified by its full_path. Re-ingesting the same full_path with a changed fingerprint is treated as a new version of that document.
2. The new version is processed and held pending, marked not current.
3. On approval, the publish transaction supersedes the previous version (Part G).
4. If the fingerprint is unchanged, the file is skipped entirely.

#### I5. Execution model

- Initial bulk load and any large migration: run from the CLI, as a background-capable job, by IT / setup. Not the UI.
- Ongoing single new or updated document: through the admin UI, self-service by HR.
- Occasional later batch: CLI, or the optional bulk UI screen if HR needs it without IT.

> **Rule:** The heavy, one-time, long-running load is a CLI job. The frequent, small, business-user task is the UI. Do not force the one-time job through a browser.

#### I6. Logs shown to the user

The principle: show progress and problems, roll up successes. A user watching a thousand-file run wants to know it is moving, how far along it is, and what needs their attention, not a thousand success lines.

##### Progress (live)

```text
Batch 8 of 20 · 400 / 1000 files · 12,400 chunks embedded · ~6 min left
```

##### Per stage (the staged summary from Part E)

- Location OK, formats found, metadata validated with error count, matched, processing, summary.

##### Per file — problems only

- Failures and quarantines are listed individually with their reason.
- Successes are rolled up as a count, not one line each.

```text
Done: 980 processed OK · 15 quarantined · 5 failed
  quarantined: old_form.pdf (password-protected), draft_v2.pdf (no metadata row) ...
  failed: corrupt_scan.pdf (unreadable) ...
```

##### End-of-run report

- Totals, timings, the full quarantine and failure list with reasons, and what is ready for approval. Written to the run report (Part E4) and shown in the UI.

### Part J — Embedding in ingestion

Embedding turns each chunk into the vector that retrieval searches. It happens at publish time, after approval, so nothing unapproved is ever embedded or searchable.

#### J1. What text is embedded

> **Rule:** The context label is embedded together with the chunk text, as one string, not the chunk text alone. A chunk reading “employees get 15 days” must be embedded with its label “India Leave Policy · Carry-over · v2024”, or it retrieves for the wrong questions.

```text
embed_input = context_label + [blank line] + chunk_text
# e.g. India Leave Policy - Carry-over - v2024 , then a blank line, then: Employees get 15 days ...
```

- The same embedding model and version are used for documents and for questions, so a question vector and a chunk vector are comparable.
- The model id and version are recorded on every chunk (embedding_model_id, embedding_version).

#### J2. Batching

- Chunks are embedded in batches, not one call per chunk. Batch size is config (default 100 chunks per call).
- Batching is what makes a thousand-document load feasible; per-chunk calls would be an order of magnitude slower.
- The embedding service is called through the shared model client, the same one retrieval uses.

```text
def embed_chunks(chunks: list[Chunk], batch_size: int = 100) -> list[EmbeddedChunk]:
    """Embed context_label + text for each chunk, in batches."""

class EmbeddedChunk(BaseModel):
    chunk_id: str; vector: list[float]
    model_id: str; model_version: str; tokens: int
```

#### J3. How the vector links to the chunk record

A chunk exists in two places. Its durable record is in Postgres; its vector and searchable payload are in Qdrant. They are linked by the same chunk_id, which is the point id in Qdrant.

| Store | Holds | Key |
|---|---|---|
| Postgres (chunk row) | chunk_id, document_id, version, heading_path, locator, parent_section_id, fingerprint, all metadata | chunk_id (primary key) |
| Qdrant (point) | the vector, plus the filterable payload (access, currency, topic) and provenance (document, version, link, download) | chunk_id (point id) |

> **Rule:** The chunk_id is generated once, in Postgres, and reused as the Qdrant point id. The chunk text lives in Qdrant with the vector; it is not duplicated into the Postgres row. Neither store holds the other's copy, so they cannot drift.

#### J4. Change detection and re-embedding

- Each chunk carries a fingerprint (a hash of its text). On re-ingestion, a chunk whose fingerprint is unchanged keeps its existing vector and is not re-embedded.
- Only new or changed chunks are embedded, so re-loading a document that changed one section does not re-embed the whole document.

> **Rule:** Changing the embedding model is different: it requires re-embedding the entire corpus, because old and new vectors are not comparable. This is not a routine operation and is flagged as such.

#### J5. Where it sits in publishing

- Embedding runs as step 1 of the publish transaction (Part G): chunks are embedded and written to the vector store marked not current, so retrieval cannot see them until approval flips them current.
- If embedding fails for a document, that document is not published and is reported; the run continues for the others.

### Part H — The ingestion UI (admin)

#### H1. Purpose and scope

- The UI is for ongoing, self-service ingestion once the system is live: an admin uploads or updates one document at a time.
- The initial bulk load of hundreds or thousands of files is NOT a UI task. It is a one-time operational job, run from the CLI by whoever sets the system up. Building UI for a one-time, hours-long job is the wrong place for it.

> **Rule:** The UI lets an admin submit content, never publish it directly. A different person approves. Self-submit, not self-publish.

#### H2. Screens

##### Single document — new or update

- Upload the file, or pick an existing document to replace with a new version.
- Machine metadata is auto-filled and shown read-only (filename, type, detected language, pages/slides).
- Human metadata entered through dropdowns bound to the vocabulary; topic is AI-suggested and confirmed.
- downloadable toggle; download_label appears when it is on.
- A preview of the extracted chunks is shown before submit, so a bad extraction is caught early.
- Submit places the document in the pending queue. It is not live.

##### Optional later batch

- If HR later receives a folder of new documents and wants to load them without IT, an optional bulk screen can drive the same staged process and show the live report. This is a convenience, not the initial-load path.
- The initial load, and any large migration, stays on the CLI.

##### Approval queue

- A reviewer sees documents pending approval, with their metadata and a content preview.
- Approve triggers embed and publish. Reject returns it with a reason.

##### Status and history

- Each document shows its state: pending, approved, live, quarantined, superseded.
- An admin can withdraw a live document; it leaves active retrieval but is kept for audit.

#### H3. UI endpoints

```text
POST /v1/admin/documents            # upload/register a single document
PUT  /v1/admin/documents/{id}       # replace with a new version
POST /v1/admin/ingestion/bulk       # start a bulk run (folder + metadata)
GET  /v1/admin/ingestion/runs/{id}  # live status + run report
GET  /v1/admin/documents/pending    # approval queue
POST /v1/admin/documents/{id}/approve
POST /v1/admin/documents/{id}/reject
POST /v1/admin/documents/{id}/withdraw
```

- All admin endpoints require the ADMIN role. Approve and reject are recorded in the audit with the approver and time.

#### H4. Config

| Setting | Owner | Value |
|---|---|---|
| supported_formats | Platform | pdf, pptx, xlsx |
| extract_service_url / auth | Platform | OCR service (Baxi Extract) — config, mockable |
| ocr_language | Platform | config-selected |
| excel_rows_per_chunk | Ingestion | default 1 (one row per chunk) |
| max_chars / overlap_pct | Ingestion | 1500 / 15 percent |
| collection | Handler | hr_policy |
| object_store_bucket | Platform | for downloadable files |
| long_run_alert_minutes | Platform | alert if a run exceeds this |

#### H5. Open items

---

# Document 3: NetraAskHRStories.xlsx

### Sheet: Sheet1

**Ask HR — Delivery Backlog (end to end)**

Full lifecycle: design through build, integration, testing (incl. Kavach), UAT, deployment, go-live and hypercare. Stage column feeds the rollup and project plan. Stream A build-now / B Baxter-dependent / C accuracy / DONE / P2 excluded from totals.

| Stream | Stage | Epic | ID | Story | What & why | Acceptance (done when) | Comp. | Type |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-01 | Migrations framework + base schema | Stand up PostgreSQL with a migrations framework so every schema change is versioned and reversible from day one, which a regulated build requires. | Migrations run clean from an empty database.<br>Any migration can be rolled back.<br>The framework is wired into app config. | Stores | Infra |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-02 | All tables from the data model | Create every table in the agreed data model — documents, versions, chunks, messages, cache, attributes and the rest — mostly generated from the spec. | All tables exist with the right columns and types.<br>Foreign keys match the data-model relationships. | Stores | Data |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-03 | Integrity rules + key indexes | Add the constraints and indexes that protect correctness and speed, especially one-current-version-per-document and the cache-invalidation/history indexes. | A document can never have two current versions.<br>History and invalidation queries use an index, not a scan. | Stores | Data |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-04 | Controlled vocabulary + seed data | Load the controlled vocabulary, the Entra-group-to-role map and starting config so access and ingestion work from first run. | Vocabulary, role map and config rows present after migration.<br>Values match what access control and ingestion expect. | Stores | Data |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-05 | Vector store provisioned | Provision Qdrant, create the HR collection with its payload schema, and index the fields that get filtered so retrieval is fast. | Collection exists with the agreed payload fields.<br>Filtered fields are indexed.<br>A vector writes, filters and reads back. | Stores | Infra |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-06 | Cache vector collection | Create the small separate collection that holds question vectors for curated answers, so semantic cache matching has an index. | Cache collection exists and is queryable by similarity. | Cache | Infra |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-07 | Config framework (nothing hardcoded) | Build the settings framework so every threshold, URL, model name and limit is config, never a literal in code — required for a GxP-auditable build. | No credential, URL, model name or threshold in code.<br>Changing a value is a config edit, not a code change. | Cross-cutting | Backend |
| A | Foundation | Foundation | FD-08 | Secret management | Set up secure handling of every credential (Entra, AI Hub, BaxiGuard, Baxi Extract, ticketing) so nothing sensitive lives in code or config files. | Secrets are referenced, never inlined.<br>Rotating a secret needs no code change. | Cross-cutting | Infra |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-01 | Ingestion framework + handler registry | Build the one ingestion framework with source-type handlers registered by type, so PDF, PPT, Excel and later schema all share the pipeline. | A handler is selected by source type.<br>Adding a type is a new handler, not a new pipeline. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-02 | PDF extractor (text) | Extract text from text-based PDFs with layout awareness (headings, tables) using PyMuPDF, locally and fast. | Text PDFs extract with structure preserved.<br>Tables are captured, not flattened. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Integration | Ingestion | IN-03 | OCR via Baxi Extract adapter | Route scanned or image-only PDFs to an external OCR service (Baxi Extract / Azure Document Intelligence) through a configurable adapter. | Scanned PDF goes to the OCR service.<br>Service URL and auth come from config.<br>The adapter is mockable for local testing. | Ingestion | Integration |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-04 | PowerPoint extractor | Parse PowerPoint natively — title, body and speaker notes — without converting to PDF, so notes and slide boundaries are kept. | Slide text and speaker notes are both captured.<br>One slide becomes one chunk. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-05 | Excel extractor (row-level) | Handle Excel as tabular data, not prose: each row a chunk with its column headers attached to every value, so meaning survives. | A row plus its headers becomes a meaningful chunk.<br>Excel is not chunked like a document. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-06 | Normalized document model | Convert every format into one normalized block structure so nothing downstream depends on the original file type. | All extractors emit the same block structure.<br>Chunking works only on blocks. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-07 | Chunking: strategy selector | Choose the chunking strategy from the document's structure — section, sliding window, table-aware or slide — rather than one fixed approach. | The strategy is selected from the blocks, not the file type. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-08 | Heading detection (PDF) | Infer headings for PDFs (which lack explicit heading styles) from font size, weight, numbering and position, with a flat-document fallback. | Headings are detected on real PDFs.<br>Documents with no headings use the sliding-window strategy. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-09 | Section chunking + size/overlap | Split long sections at paragraph boundaries within a size cap, with sentence-level overlap so a chunk never starts mid-sentence. | Long sections split at paragraphs.<br>Overlap is whole sentences, never mid-sentence. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-10 | Tables & lists handling | Keep tables and lists intact — a table is one chunk with its header repeated; a list stays with its introducing line. | Tables are not split; header repeated.<br>Lists are kept with their lead-in. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-11 | Parent-child + context label | Keep the parent section for each chunk and prepend a one-line context label, the single biggest retrieval-quality gain. | Each chunk carries its parent id and a context label. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-12 | Metadata file loader + validator | Read the human metadata file, validate every controlled value against the vocabulary, and key each row to its file by full path. | Invalid values are named by field.<br>Duplicate paths error.<br>Files with no row are quarantined. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-13 | Metadata model + fan-out | Assemble machine + human + system metadata and fan the human fields out onto every chunk, since retrieval filters at chunk level. | Every chunk carries access, currency and topic metadata. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-14 | Downloadable documents | Handle downloadable documents — copy the file to the object store, serve a link, and show it alongside the citation in answers. | Downloadable file is served from the object store.<br>The download link appears with the citation. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-15 | Bulk load: staged pipeline + CLI | Run the bulk load as a staged, fully-logged process driven by a CLI with flags (folder, metadata, recursive, dry-run). | Each stage validates and logs before the next.<br>Dry-run reports without writing anything. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-16 | Batching + checkpointing + idempotency | Batch the work, checkpoint progress and skip unchanged files by fingerprint, so a large load is fast and resumable. | A crash at file 400 resumes at 401.<br>Unchanged files are skipped; no duplicates. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-17 | Embedding in ingestion (batched) | Embed chunks in batches, embedding the context label with the text, and link each vector to its chunk record by id. | Chunks are embedded in batches.<br>The vector point id equals the chunk id. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-18 | Approve + publish transaction | Implement the approve-and-publish transaction so nothing is searchable before approval and there is never a two-version window. | Nothing searchable before approval.<br>No window where two versions answer or none does. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | Ingestion | IN-19 | Quarantine + run report | Quarantine failures with reasons and report processed/skipped/quarantined/published counts for every run. | Failures listed with reasons.<br>A run report is produced. | Ingestion | Backend |
| B | Integration | Ingestion | IN-20 | SharePoint / Employee Connect source | Connect read-only to SharePoint / Employee Connect to list and pull the source documents for ingestion. | Documents are listed and pulled.<br>The source platform is not modified. | Ingestion | Integration |
| A | Build | Ingestion UI | UI-01 | Single upload / update screen | Admin screen to upload or update a single document: machine metadata auto-filled, human metadata via dropdowns, a chunk preview, then submit for approval. | Document uploaded, tagged, previewed and submitted pending.<br>It is not live until approved. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Ingestion UI | UI-02 | Bulk screen with live run report | Bulk screen that runs the staged process behind a live report so an admin can load a folder and see stage-by-stage results. | Stage log and per-file results shown live.<br>Quarantined files listed with reasons. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Ingestion UI | UI-03 | Approval queue | Approval queue where a reviewer sees pending documents with metadata and a preview and approves or rejects. | Approve publishes; reject returns with a reason. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Ingestion UI | UI-04 | Document status & withdraw | Show each document's state and let an admin withdraw a live document, which leaves retrieval but is kept for audit. | States shown per document.<br>Withdraw removes from retrieval, keeps the audit record. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Ingestion UI | UI-05 | Admin ingestion endpoints | Build the admin ingestion endpoints behind the UI, all role-protected and audited. | Endpoints work; ADMIN role enforced.<br>Approvals are recorded in the audit. | API | Backend |
| DONE | Build | Access | AC-01 | Entra SSO (already integrated) | Sign-in via Entra. | Signed-in user reaches the service; group claims present. | Access | Integration |
| A | Build | Access | AC-02 | Access resolution chain | Resolve who the user is and what content they may see through the chain: token claims, then directory, then a local table, then a safe default. | Roles and attributes resolve for a normal user.<br>No attributes means general-audience only.<br>The source used is recorded. | Access | Backend |
| A | Build | Access | AC-03 | Attribute → retrieval filter + vocab map | Turn the user's attributes into a retrieval filter via the controlled vocabulary; an unmappable value is treated as general. | Search is filtered to the user's audience plus general.<br>Unmappable values are logged. | Access | Backend |
| A | Build | Access | AC-04 | Session attribute cache | Hold resolved attributes for the session with a short expiry, so the directory is not called on every question. | Attributes reused within the session.<br>They refresh on token refresh and expire on time. | Access | Backend |
| B | Integration | Access | AC-05 | Microsoft Graph directory lookup | Fetch attributes not present in the token from Microsoft Graph, once at sign-in. | Missing attributes fetched from the directory once per session. | Access | Integration |
| A | Build | Retrieval | RT-01 | Embed query once, reuse | Embed the question once and reuse the vector for both cache lookup and retrieval, so the same question is never embedded twice. | One embedding call per question.<br>The same vector feeds cache and search. | Retrieval | Backend |
| A | Build | Retrieval | RT-02 | Hybrid search + deterministic filter | Run hybrid (dense + sparse) search with the deterministic filter (current, in-force, audience) applied inside the search. | Only current, audience-appropriate content is returned.<br>Exact terms like a policy name match reliably. | Retrieval | Backend |
| A | Build | Retrieval | RT-03 | Grade + corrective retrieval | Grade the retrieved material and, if weak, reword the question and search again up to twice rather than answering from poor material. | Weak retrieval triggers a reworded retry, capped at two.<br>Still weak returns no answer, not a guess. | Retrieval | Backend |
| A | Build | Retrieval | RT-04 | Rerank + parent expansion | Re-score candidates with a cross-encoder, keep the best few, and expand each to its parent section for enough context. | Best sections kept and expanded.<br>Reranker down degrades gracefully with a confidence penalty. | Retrieval | Backend |
| A | Build | Retrieval | RT-05 | retrieval_strength + evidence by code | Compute retrieval strength and capture the evidence deterministically in code, so every answer traces to real chunks. | Strength recorded per request.<br>Evidence is captured by code, not the model. | Retrieval | Backend |
| A | Build | Answer | RT-06 | Answer generation (grounded, cited) | Generate a grounded, cited answer using only the retrieved evidence, declining when the evidence does not cover the question. | Uses only the evidence.<br>Cites the sections used.<br>Declines when not covered. | Answer service | Backend |
| A | Build | Answer | RT-07 | Confidence scoring | Score answer confidence from retrieval strength and groundedness and withhold the answer below the bar. | Below threshold the answer is withheld.<br>Confidence is computed, not the model's self-rating. | Answer service | Backend |
| A | Build | Answer | RT-08 | HR domain handler | Implement the HR domain handler that supplies retrieval config and passes context through as evidence, keeping HR knowledge in one place. | Supplies configuration, not control flow.<br>The orchestrator works against it via the interface. | Domain handler | Backend |
| B | Integration | Answer | RT-09 | LLM gateway (AI Hub) + model routing | Integrate the answer model (Claude via Baxter AI Hub) through the gateway, with cheap/main model routing by config. | Answer model reachable through the gateway.<br>Model and version recorded per request. | Model services | Integration |
| A | Integration | Model services | MS-01 | Embedding model serving | Serve the self-hosted embedding model on GPU with batching, warm-up and a health check. | Serves embeddings with batching.<br>Warm at start; reports health. | Model services | Infra |
| A | Integration | Model services | MS-02 | Reranker serving | Serve the self-hosted reranker on GPU with a health check. | Scores candidates on demand.<br>Its absence degrades gracefully. | Model services | Infra |
| B | Integration | Model services | MS-03 | Baxter AI Hub access | Complete Baxter AI Hub access approvals and wiring, which depend on an external team. | Access approved and working end to end. | Model services | Integration |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-01 | Supervisor + agent registry (HR) | Build the supervisor and agent registry with the HR agent registered and a scope description, so adding an agent later is a registration. | Routes to HR; returns a confidence; logged.<br>Adding an agent is registration, not a rewrite. | Router | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-02 | Deterministic-first route + thresholds | Route deterministically first from hard signals, falling back to a bounded LLM that can only pick a registered agent, with score+gap thresholds. | A rule decides where it can.<br>Thresholds decide confident/ambiguous/none. | Router | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-03 | History rewrite | Rewrite a follow-up into a standalone question using the last few turns of the current conversation, before routing. | A follow-up is rewritten to standalone.<br>The first question in a thread skips it. | Router | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-04 | Greeting / junk short-circuit | Catch greetings and junk with a cheap check and reply directly, skipping the whole pipeline. | A greeting is answered instantly.<br>The pipeline is skipped. | Router | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-05 | Conversation model (threaded + session) | Implement the threaded conversation model with a session window and topic boundary, so history is pulled only from the active thread. | History pulled from the active thread only.<br>Idle timeout starts a new thread. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-06 | Clarify / disambiguation flow | When confidence is low, ask one specific clarifying question and offer a re-route, never 'refine your query'. | Ambiguous asks one specific question.<br>A re-route control is offered. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-07 | Streaming + progress events | Stream progress events per step; generate the answer in full, screen it, then stream it to the display. | Progress shown as each step runs.<br>Answer screened before any of it is shown. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Routing & chat | RC-08 | Partial results + timeout + cancel | Show partial results when one of several agents fails, enforce a 30-second deadline, and let the user cancel an in-flight question. | Partial answer shown with a note.<br>Deadline enforced; cancel works. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Orchestration | OR-01 | Orchestrator + request state | Build the orchestrator with one request-state object, step sequencing and the short-circuit points. | Runs end to end; state accumulates.<br>Short circuits return the right outcome and still log. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Orchestration | OR-02 | Failure mapping + error catalogue | Map every internal failure to one of the five response states with an error code, so nothing surfaces as a raw exception. | Each failure resolves to a defined state and code.<br>No internal error reaches the user. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Orchestration | OR-03 | Token economics recording | Record tokens and cost at every step and aggregate per request, including retries and cost avoided on a cache hit. | Every call's tokens and cost recorded.<br>Per-request total includes retries and cost avoided. | Cross-cutting | Backend |
| A | Build | Orchestration | OR-04 | Public /v1/ask + endpoints | Expose the public /v1/ask endpoint and the supporting endpoints (history, feedback, tickets, metrics). | Answer returned with sources.<br>Handled outcomes return 200 with a status. | API | Backend |
| A | Build | Orchestration | OR-05 | Rate limiting | Cap questions per user per period to protect against a stuck tab or a loop, without affecting normal use. | Over-limit gets a clear response.<br>The limit is generous enough that no real user meets it. | API | Backend |
| A | Integration | Guardrails | GD-01 | BaxiGuard adapter (in/out screening) | Screen questions and answers through BaxiGuard via a configurable adapter for injection, unsafe content and personal data. | Injection and PII are blocked.<br>Blocked input or output returns a refusal.<br>Service URL is config. | Guardrail | Integration |
| A | Build | Cache | CA-01 | Curated answer cache (lookup) | Serve curated, human-approved answers to common questions by semantic match, keyed on the access scope, so repeats cost no model call. | A repeat curated question returns without a model call.<br>Matching is scoped by access. | Cache | Backend |
| A | Build | Cache | CA-02 | Curated answers admin + approval trail | Let an admin create, edit, approve and retire curated answers, each with an author/approver/date trail. | A curated answer records author, approver and time.<br>Retiring one removes it from serving. | Cache | Backend |
| A | Build | Cache | CA-03 | Cache invalidation on republish | Invalidate curated answers built on a policy version when that version is superseded, so no cached answer cites a retired policy. | Superseding a version deactivates dependent curated answers. | Cache | Backend |
| A | Integration | Ticketing | TK-01 | Provider-neutral ticketing adapter | Build the provider-neutral ticketing adapter so the target system (Jira/ServiceNow) is configuration. | A ticket is created in the configured system.<br>Changing provider is configuration only. | Ticketing | Integration |
| A | Integration | Ticketing | TK-02 | Offer/confirm + duplicate prevention | Offer a ticket when the system cannot answer, create it only on confirmation, and prevent duplicates. | A ticket is offered, not raised silently.<br>Confirming twice does not duplicate. | Ticketing | Backend |
| A | Build | Audit | AU-01 | Audit record + durable queue (fail-closed) | Assemble one audit record per request and write it to a durable queue before responding, failing the request if it cannot be recorded. | Every outcome produces an audit record.<br>No answer returned if it could not be recorded. | Orchestrator | Backend |
| A | Build | Audit | AU-02 | Append-only audit + history view | Make audit records append-only and expose chat history as a user-scoped, time-windowed view of the same rows. | Audit records cannot be edited or deleted.<br>Chat history is the user's own recent view of the same rows. | Audit | Backend |
| A | Build | Audit | AU-03 | Retention + archive tiers | Apply separate retention for chat history and audit, moving old records to compressed archive after the live window. | Live database stays flat.<br>Archive fills to the retention limit then stops. | Audit | Backend |
| A | Build | Frontend | FE-01 | Ask screen + cited answers + streaming | Build the ask screen with the streamed, cited answer and a subtle source label. | Answer shown with citations and a source label.<br>Progress is shown while it runs. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Frontend | FE-02 | Conversation history view | Show the user their own recent conversations within the history window. | Sees own history only.<br>Within the retention window only. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Frontend | FE-03 | No-answer + ticket offer + downloads | Show the no-answer state with a ticket offer, HR contacts, and download links where relevant. | No-answer offers a ticket and contacts.<br>Download links shown for downloadable sources. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Frontend | FE-04 | Feedback control | Add thumbs up/down with an optional comment on every answer. | Any answer can be rated.<br>A comment can be added. | UI | Frontend |
| A | Build | Frontend | FE-05 | Usage & cost dashboard (admin) | Give admins a dashboard of usage, cost, cache hit rate and quality metrics. | Cost per question and cache hit rate shown.<br>No-answer and error rates visible. | UI | Frontend |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-01 | Golden test set from tickets | Build the golden test set from real HR tickets — questions with approved answers and expected sources, including unanswerable ones. | Real questions with approved answers and sources.<br>Unanswerable questions included. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-02 | Eval harness (retrieval/answer/safety) | Build the evaluation harness that scores retrieval, answer and safety against the test set on every change. | Scores retrieval, answer and safety.<br>Runs on demand and on change. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-03 | Second-model grader + calibration | Use a second model to grade groundedness and relevance at scale, checked against human judgement on a sample. | Grader scores at scale.<br>Agrees with human review on a sample. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-04 | Threshold tuning | Tune the grade, confidence and routing thresholds against the test set once real scores are visible. | Thresholds set from real scores.<br>The launch bar is met on the test set. | Evaluation | Test |
| A | Testing | Evaluation | EV-05 | Unit tests (backend) | Cover the backend components with unit tests that run in the pipeline. | Core logic covered.<br>Tests run in CI. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| A | Testing | Evaluation | EV-06 | Integration tests (stub handler) | Test the whole flow end to end against a stub handler with no HR content, proving the orchestrator holds no domain knowledge. | Flow passes with a stub handler.<br>No HR content needed for the test. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-07 | Access-control tests | Write access-control tests that prove restricted content is never retrieved for an unauthorised user. | Audience filtering proven.<br>Restricted content never returned to the wrong user. | Access | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-08 | Guardrail / poisoning tests | Test that prompt injection and instructions hidden in a document are ignored. | A poisoned document does not change behaviour. | Guardrail | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-09 | Load / performance test | Test the system under expected peak concurrency against the latency targets. | Meets latency targets at peak concurrency. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| C | Testing | Evaluation | EV-10 | UAT with HR pilot | Run user acceptance testing with the HR pilot group and act on the findings. | Pilot signs off.<br>Findings logged and addressed. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| B | Deployment | Deployment | DP-01 | Environments (dev/qa/staging/prod) | Stand up dev, QA, staging and production environments once hosting is decided. | Environments isolated.<br>Config differs per environment; code does not. | DevOps | Infra |
| B | Deployment | Deployment | DP-02 | CI/CD pipeline | Build the CI/CD pipeline that tests and ships code through the environments. | A change runs tests and deploys.<br>A failing gate blocks the deploy. | DevOps | Infra |
| A | Deployment | Deployment | DP-03 | Observability dashboards + alerts | Surface the defined metrics as dashboards and set alerts, including alerts on long or failed ingestion runs. | Cost, quality and error dashboards exist.<br>Alerts fire on anomalies and ingestion failures. | DevOps | Infra |
| A | Deployment | Deployment | DP-04 | Backup & restore (Postgres + Qdrant) | Set up backup and tested restore for both PostgreSQL and Qdrant. | Backups run on schedule.<br>A restore has been tested, not just configured. | DevOps | Infra |
| A | Deployment | Deployment | DP-05 | Runbook + support handover | Write the runbook and agree the support model before go-live. | Runbook covers the common failures.<br>Support ownership agreed. | DevOps | Infra |
| P2 | Phase 2 | Phase 2 | P2-01 | Ticket-to-curated-answer loop | When HR answers a ticket the system could not, turn that question and approved answer into a curated entry through the same gate. | An answered ticket can become a curated answer.<br>It goes through the same approval trail. | Cache | Backend |
| P2 | Phase 2 | Phase 2 | P2-02 | Intranet (ASPX) source | Add intranet (ASPX) pages as a source, pulling content from a known list. | Intranet pages ingested from a known list. | Ingestion | Backend |
| P2 | Phase 2 | Phase 2 | P2-03 | Video via captions | Add selected videos as a source using their existing captions. | Captioned videos ingested for a chosen set. | Ingestion | Backend |
| A | Build | RBAC | RB-01 | Role model & permission matrix | Define the roles (Employee, Admin, HR content owner, Auditor) and a matrix mapping each role to allowed actions. | Roles defined with a clear permission matrix.<br>Every capability maps to a role. | Access | Backend |
| A | Integration | RBAC | RB-02 | Entra group to app-role mapping | Map Entra AD groups to application roles via configuration, so role assignment lives in Entra, not the app. | Entra groups resolve to app roles.<br>Mapping is configuration, not code. | Access | Backend |
| A | Build | RBAC | RB-03 | Role enforcement on every endpoint | Enforce the required role on each API endpoint and UI action, so admin and ingestion functions are protected from day one. | A non-admin is rejected from admin endpoints.<br>Every endpoint checks its required role. | Access | Backend |
| A | Build | RBAC | RB-04 | Content-level access hooks (attribute RBAC) | Wire the attribute content filter to the role model so restricted content can be enforced when it exists; mechanism now, enforcement Phase 2. | Access scope carried on every request and cache key.<br>Mechanism testable though all docs are general in Phase 1. | Access | Backend |
| A | Build | RBAC | RB-05 | Auditor role (read-only audit) | Add a read-only Auditor role that can view the audit trail and nothing else. | Auditor reads the audit; cannot edit or delete.<br>No content-management actions for Auditor. | Audit | Backend |
| A | Build | RBAC | RB-06 | Role admin & assignment audit | Let an admin view effective roles and record every role change in the audit. | Effective role visible per user.<br>Role changes are audited. | Access | Backend |
| C | Testing | Testing (Kavach) | KV-01 | Author testable requirements for Kavach | Convert acceptance criteria into requirement statements Kavach can generate test cases from. | Requirements in the form Kavach consumes.<br>Cover functional, accuracy and safety. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | Testing | Testing (Kavach) | KV-02 | Generate test cases via Kavach | Feed the requirements to Kavach and generate the test-case set for the answer path and ingestion. | Test cases generated from requirements.<br>Reviewed for coverage. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | Testing | Testing (Kavach) | KV-03 | Execute & track Kavach test runs | Run the generated tests through Kavach, track pass/fail, and route defects back. | Runs executed and tracked.<br>Defects raised and linked. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | Testing | Testing (Kavach) | KV-04 | Regression suite in Kavach | Maintain a regression suite in Kavach that runs on every significant change. | Regression suite runs on change.<br>A regression blocks release. | Evaluation | Test |
| C | UAT | UAT | UA-01 | UAT plan & scenarios | Prepare the UAT plan and business scenarios with the HR pilot group. | UAT plan agreed with HR.<br>Scenarios cover real HR questions. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| C | UAT | UAT | UA-02 | Run UAT with HR pilot | Run UAT sessions with the pilot group; capture feedback and issues. | Pilot completes the scenarios.<br>Feedback and issues logged. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| C | UAT | UAT | UA-03 | UAT fixes & sign-off | Address UAT findings and obtain formal sign-off. | Findings addressed.<br>HR signs off to proceed. | Cross-cutting | Test |
| A | Deployment | Training & docs | TR-01 | Employee user guide | Write a short guide for employees on using Ask HR. | Guide covers asking, reading answers, sources, tickets. | UI | Backend |
| A | Deployment | Training & docs | TR-02 | Admin & content-owner guide | Write the guide for admins and HR content owners on ingestion, approval and curated answers. | Guide covers upload, metadata, approval, curated answers. | UI | Backend |
| A | Deployment | Training & docs | TR-03 | Support runbook & FAQ | Prepare the support runbook and an internal FAQ for the support team. | Runbook covers common failures.<br>FAQ covers common user questions. | DevOps | Backend |
| A | Deployment | Training & docs | TR-04 | Training sessions for HR & support | Run training sessions for HR content owners and the support team. | HR owners trained on content management.<br>Support team trained on triage. | Cross-cutting | Backend |

| B | Go-live | Go-live | GL-02 | Production cutover & smoke test | Deploy to production, load approved content, run smoke tests. | Production live with approved content.<br>Smoke tests pass. | DevOps | Infra |
| B | Go-live | Go-live | GL-03 | Rollback plan & drill | Prepare and rehearse the rollback plan. | Rollback documented.<br>Rollback rehearsed successfully. | DevOps | Infra |
| B | Hypercare | Hypercare | HC-01 | Hypercare support window | Intensive support for the first weeks after go-live: monitor, triage, fix fast, daily check-ins. | Issues triaged and fixed quickly.<br>Daily check-ins held.<br>Metrics watched closely. | DevOps | Infra |
| B | Hypercare | Hypercare | HC-02 | Post-go-live tuning | Tune thresholds, cache and content on real early usage. | Thresholds and curated set tuned on real usage.<br>No-answer gaps fed back to HR. | Evaluation | Test |
